Findings

Requested shot count not honoured

The reference and target complete a different number of shots than requested. Both sides agree, so the port preserves this behaviour; whether the request should be honoured is a review decision. Seen in 36 checks across 18 executions — one grouped change, not 36 independent bugs.

Model disclosure

Statements mentioning shots:

  • Removed the `nshots=512` argument from `backend.run(...)`. It is not an Aer option (the option is `shots`), so it was most likely ignored before. Removing it should not change behaviour, but it is a code change.
  • Deliberately did NOT 'fix' the shots handling. `backend.shots = shots` is kept as a plain attribute with no effect on execution, and the `shots` argument still does not control the sample count. Runs use the Aer default of 1024 shots, as in the source (assuming the old `nshots` kwarg was ignored).
All 4 stated changes
  • Import changed: `from qiskit import QuantumCircuit, Aer` became `from qiskit import QuantumCircuit` plus `from qiskit_aer import AerSimulator`, because `qiskit.Aer` no longer exists in Qiskit 2.x.
  • Backend is now `AerSimulator()` (default method) instead of `Aer.get_backend('qasm_simulator')`. The legacy QasmSimulator is a different class. Sampling semantics should be the same, but this is a different backend object.
  • Removed the `nshots=512` argument from `backend.run(...)`. It is not an Aer option (the option is `shots`), so it was most likely ignored before. Removing it should not change behaviour, but it is a code change.
  • Deliberately did NOT 'fix' the shots handling. `backend.shots = shots` is kept as a plain attribute with no effect on execution, and the `shots` argument still does not control the sample count. Runs use the Aer default of 1024 shots, as in the source (assuming the old `nshots` kwarg was ignored).

Model-stated intent (claude-sonnet-5-5). It is not an observation and not human approval.

Human decision

Not recorded. Copy review-template.json, fill in a reviewer, decision and reason, then run qref report --decisions. QRef never infers acceptance from model notes.

Coverage and limits

Checked

Actually submitted circuits (equivalence), measurement map and dimensions, exact output probabilities, independent exact objective, module helpers on every basis assignment, module weighting of captured counts and exact probabilities, returned values against their own histograms, and requested versus completed shots.

Not checked

  • optimizer convergence
  • hardware execution
  • noise models
  • dynamic circuits
  • inputs outside the declared graphs and angles
  • whole-program equivalence

Limits

  • Checks cover only the declared graphs, angles and shot requests.
  • Numerical agreement uses floating-point arithmetic with absolute tolerance 1e-10.
  • No optimizer convergence, hardware, noise, dynamic-circuit or whole-program claim.
  • The reference is a preservation baseline, not scientific truth.
  • Candidate code ran locally as trusted, inspected code; virtual environments isolate versions, not hostile code.
  • Hashes show internal consistency; they are not a signature or an independent attestation.

Unified diff from reference.py to target.py. Hunks that mention shots come first. + lines were added, - lines removed.

Full reference source
from qiskit import QuantumCircuit, Aer
import networkx as nx

def maxcut_obj(x, G):
    """
    Given a bitstring as a solution, this function returns
    the number of edges shared between the two partitions
    of the graph.
    
    Args:
        x: str
           solution bitstring
           
        G: networkx graph
        
    Returns:
        obj: float
             Objective
    """
    obj = 0
    for i, j in G.edges():
        if x[i] != x[j]:
            obj -= 1
            
    return obj


def compute_expectation(counts, G):
    
    """
    Computes expectation value based on measurement results
    
    Args:
        counts: dict
                key as bitstring, val as count
           
        G: networkx graph
        
    Returns:
        avg: float
             expectation value
    """
    
    avg = 0
    sum_count = 0
    for bitstring, count in counts.items():
        
        obj = maxcut_obj(bitstring[::-1], G)
        avg += obj * count
        sum_count += count
        
    return avg/sum_count


# We will also bring the different circuit components that
# build the qaoa circuit under a single function
def create_qaoa_circ(G, theta):
    
    """
    Creates a parametrized qaoa circuit
    
    Args:  
        G: networkx graph
        theta: list
               unitary parameters
                     
    Returns:
        qc: qiskit circuit
    """
    
    nqubits = len(G.nodes())
    p = len(theta)//2  # number of alternating unitaries
    qc = QuantumCircuit(nqubits)
    
    beta = theta[:p]
    gamma = theta[p:]
    
    # initial_state
    for i in range(0, nqubits):
        qc.h(i)
    
    for irep in range(0, p):
        
        # problem unitary
        for pair in list(G.edges()):
            qc.rzz(2 * gamma[irep], pair[0], pair[1])

        # mixer unitary
        for i in range(0, nqubits):
            qc.rx(2 * beta[irep], i)
            
    qc.measure_all()
        
    return qc

# Finally we write a function that executes the circuit on the chosen backend
def get_expectation(G, shots=512):
    
    """
    Runs parametrized circuit
    
    Args:
        G: networkx graph
        p: int,
           Number of repetitions of unitaries
    """
    
    backend = Aer.get_backend('qasm_simulator')
    backend.shots = shots
    
    def execute_circ(theta):
        
        qc = create_qaoa_circ(G, theta)
        counts = backend.run(qc, seed_simulator=10, 
                             nshots=512).result().get_counts()
        
        return compute_expectation(counts, G)
    
    return execute_circ
Full target source
from qiskit import QuantumCircuit
from qiskit_aer import AerSimulator
import networkx as nx

def maxcut_obj(x, G):
    """
    Given a bitstring as a solution, this function returns
    the number of edges shared between the two partitions
    of the graph.
    
    Args:
        x: str
           solution bitstring
           
        G: networkx graph
        
    Returns:
        obj: float
             Objective
    """
    obj = 0
    for i, j in G.edges():
        if x[i] != x[j]:
            obj -= 1
            
    return obj


def compute_expectation(counts, G):
    
    """
    Computes expectation value based on measurement results
    
    Args:
        counts: dict
                key as bitstring, val as count
           
        G: networkx graph
        
    Returns:
        avg: float
             expectation value
    """
    
    avg = 0
    sum_count = 0
    for bitstring, count in counts.items():
        
        obj = maxcut_obj(bitstring[::-1], G)
        avg += obj * count
        sum_count += count
        
    return avg/sum_count


# We will also bring the different circuit components that
# build the qaoa circuit under a single function
def create_qaoa_circ(G, theta):
    
    """
    Creates a parametrized qaoa circuit
    
    Args:  
        G: networkx graph
        theta: list
               unitary parameters
                     
    Returns:
        qc: qiskit circuit
    """
    
    nqubits = len(G.nodes())
    p = len(theta)//2  # number of alternating unitaries
    qc = QuantumCircuit(nqubits)
    
    beta = theta[:p]
    gamma = theta[p:]
    
    # initial_state
    for i in range(0, nqubits):
        qc.h(i)
    
    for irep in range(0, p):
        
        # problem unitary
        for pair in list(G.edges()):
            qc.rzz(2 * gamma[irep], pair[0], pair[1])

        # mixer unitary
        for i in range(0, nqubits):
            qc.rx(2 * beta[irep], i)
            
    qc.measure_all()
        
    return qc

# Finally we write a function that executes the circuit on the chosen backend
def get_expectation(G, shots=512):
    
    """
    Runs parametrized circuit
    
    Args:
        G: networkx graph
        p: int,
           Number of repetitions of unitaries
    """
    
    backend = AerSimulator()
    backend.shots = shots
    
    def execute_circ(theta):
        
        qc = create_qaoa_circ(G, theta)
        counts = backend.run(qc, seed_simulator=10).result().get_counts()
        
        return compute_expectation(counts, G)
    
    return execute_circ
Model summary and stated uncertainties

Replaced the removed `qiskit.Aer` import and the 'qasm_simulator' lookup with `qiskit_aer.AerSimulator()`. Dropped the unrecognised `nshots=512` run kwarg. Circuit construction, MaxCut sign, bitstring reversal, and expectation computation are unchanged. Nothing was executed.

  • I assumed the old `nshots=512` kwarg was silently ignored in aer 0.14.2, so the effective shot count was the default 1024, not 512. If it was honoured in some way, the old counts differ from the new ones. Please confirm which is intended. If you want `shots` honoured, use `backend.run(qc, shots=shots, seed_simulator=10)`. That would be a behaviour change.
  • With `seed_simulator=10`, exact counts and expectation values may differ from 0.14.2 because the RNG, backend internals, or default simulation method can change between aer versions. Statistical behaviour should match, but bit-for-bit reproduction is not guaranteed.
  • Nodes are assumed to be labelled 0..n-1, as in the source. Other labels would fail in `rzz` and in bitstring indexing, exactly as before.
  • I did not add an explicit `transpile` step. I believe Aer 0.18 accepts h, rx, rzz and measure directly, but I could not run it to verify.
